The candidate set also includes selections of ≤ 16 faces of any type: freeform surfaces bait the analytic detectors locally (accidentally-coplanar strip pairs at wave inflections, 6-face cylinder ribbons carving up the saddle, 9–12-face cones on a Gaussian bump peak), and left selected they punch holes in the region and leave fake feature edges. Two guards protect real small features: a component with no single-face planar member is never fitted, and when absorbed non-single faces would outnumber the single-face leftovers the component is stripped back to the leftovers.
```
417	2. **Tangent-plane merge**: a multi-face planar selection whose *entire* shared boundary with a component is smooth is the flat part of the same freeform surface (B-spline local support makes exactly-planar margins — bspline_blister) and is merged in; keeping it separate invents a feature edge the reference doesn't have. Real planar neighbors (walls, bottoms) always meet freeform regions across at least some sharp edges. Analytic models are untouched — they have no ≥ 4-face freeform components.
418	3. Components below 4 faces stay single-face planar. Components planar to fitting exactness (10× vertex tolerance, NaN-guarded: the area-weighted normal of a closed region cancels to zero) become a multi-face planar hypothesis instead — a plane is preferable to a B-spline that overfits noise.
419	4. **Parameterize** the region onto the unit square: outer boundary loop pinned by cumulative chord length, 4 corners chosen where the adjacent *outside* selection changes (arc-length-quartile fallback when the boundary offers ≠ 4 transitions), interior and hole-loop vertices solved with Floater mean-value weights by Gauss–Seidel (convergence enforced — an unconverged map voids Floater's bijectivity guarantee). Quads are split along the v0–v2 diagonal for weighting and sampling. **Closed bands** (a second boundary loop of extent ≥ half the largest — a small hole floats freely, a comparable second loop means the region wraps, e.g. the wall of a closed prism) are first **seam-cut** into a disk along a shortest vertex path between the two loops (\`cut_region_seam\`): the path follows FACE perimeter edges only (a path along a quad's split diagonal would tear that quad across the cut), its vertices are duplicated, right-side triangle fans switch to the clones, and the boundary re-chains into one loop whose two cut copies carry a \`CUT_OUTSIDE\` id — corner detection then lands the 4 corners exactly at the cut endpoints. The clones keep their originals' positions, so the two parameter-extreme grid columns of the around-the-band axis carry identical data (the periodic fit drops the closing one; the clamped fallback fit keeps both and closes exactly through the shared column).
420	5. **Resample a grid** through the flattened triangulation (uniform-bin point location; numeric misses near corner slivers are clamped to the nearest triangle within one bin — feeding them to the hole membrane extrapolates garbage). Grid stations per axis are the boundary vertices' own pinned positions (stations *between* the vertices of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the fit to the chordal prism — a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own chordal error), evenly subdivided up to an interior-density target of ~1.5·√(region vertices) (a flat margin tessellates coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on a 946-vertex region). Genuine hole interiors are filled with a Laplacian membrane so the fitter sees smooth data. The grid is flipped if needed so d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`.
421	6. **Fit**: \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate\` (chord-length, C2 cubic) first — the grid is sampled from an exact-on-surface mesh, and OCCT's least-squares approximator reports \`IsDone\` while overshooting its \`Tol3D\` by millimeters near strongly curved patch boundaries. The approximator (tol = surface/8, then surface/2) remains as fallback, and grid density retries at caps 121/181/241. **Closed bands fit periodically**: \`Interpolate(…, thePeriodic=true)\` with the around-the-band axis as the array's FIRST index (transposed when that axis is v; the outward-orientation flip then reverses the second index and accounts for the transpose's normal negation) and the closing station omitted — OCCT appends the wrap-around copy of the first row itself and calls \`SetUPeriodic()\`. The periodic path is interpolation-only (the approximator binding carries no periodic flag). The resulting closure is C1, not C2 (position and D1 match across the wrap to ~1e-14; D2 jumps — measured), which nothing downstream requires. The fitted around-axis knots are then rescaled to exactly [0, 2π] via \`SetUKnots\` — an exact affine reparameterization (~1e-15 drift, measured) — because stage 3.4's wrapped-face machinery hard-codes that period. The U=0 iso is then exactly the cut path (chord-length parameters start at the first supplied row), a free seam gauge.
422	7. **Validate**: every region vertex and face centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\` of the fit, each projection seeded from the vertex's own parameterization value (sequential seeding breaks at mesh row wraps, and neither \`NextValueOfUV\` nor \`ValueOfUV\` recovers reliably on many-knot surfaces). On failure the component stays single-face planar with a warning.
423	8. **Closed-band result**: the periodic fit produces ONE \`BSplineHypothesis\` with \`u_periodic = true\`, reconstructed by stage 3 as one wrapped face with a real seam edge — the form CAD kernels (and the references) write. If the periodic fit fails validation at every grid density, the band retries with the clamped fit and is **seam-split** (\`split_cut_hypothesis\`) into two \`SelectedSurface::BSpline\` halves sharing the fitted surface object; their two cut edges are plain edges that a reference model does not have, so the stage 3.1 edge compare will (correctly) flag them — the fallback trades compare-exactness for robustness and is currently exercised by no model.
424	
425	The \`BSplineHypothesis\` owns the fitted \`Geom_BSplineSurface\` (stage 3 reuses the same object via fresh handles) plus a cached, seed-rechecking \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` projector used by absorption, refinement, \`--compare\`, and stage-3 charts/normals/projections. Stage-3 charts of B-spline faces also carry a parameter-space \`home\` anchor (the projection of the region's mean face centroid): a projection landing on a coincident-boundary curve of a seam-split band (the fallback) is ambiguous between the two parameter extremes (Newton, the gap re-check, and \`ValueOfUV\` all cannot tell — both branches are exact), and the branch nearer the face's own interior wins. On a periodic (u_periodic) chart the U-arm of that resolution is replaced by principal-value reduction to [0, 2π) — the same contract as the analytic periodic charts — and the whole wrapped-face machinery (edge-sample branch unwrap, winding classification, seam planning/anchoring, pcurve shifts) applies verbatim. There is no compare exemption: the stage 3.1 edge/vertex compare is fully armed for every edge, including the seam (a real feature both the reconstruction and the reference have).
426	
427	**Periodic-band junctions (stage 3.3)**: the band's two cap junctions are closed-loop edges (no vertices) and must carry PERIODIC curves — seam planning re-anchors a closed edge with \`MakeEdge\` over [t, t+period] at a solved seam azimuth, which a closed-but-non-periodic curve refuses. Two mechanisms deliver that (both were needed on elliptical_cylinder): (a) an IntSS curve that comes back closed-but-non-periodic (observed type: \`Geom_TrimmedCurve\`, exact to ~1e-14) is converted via \`GeomConvert::CurveToBSplineCurve\` + \`SetPeriodic\` (exact, measured); (b) an IntSS curve that comes back as a genuine partial arc falls to the boundary-chain repair, whose closed-chain arm interpolates a periodic C2 B-spline through the chain vertices (\`GeomAPI_Interpolate\` with the periodic flag, closing point not repeated — ~4e-15 chain deviation observed). Partial-arc detection for closed loops keys on the ENDPOINT GAP, not the parameter span: a closed B-spline junction arrives over an arbitrary range (observed [0,1]), and the old span≠2π test replaced the true junction with a bogus circle (14.8 mm off, 12% volume error).
```

```
/// Grid stations for one axis. The base stations are the boundary
/// vertices' own pinned positions (averaged between the two opposite
/// sides — structured or not, the boundary is where the region has exact
/// on-surface data at exact parameters): stations *between* the vertices
/// of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the interpolant to
/// the chordal prism (a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own
/// chordal error). The base is then evenly subdivided up to the interior-
/// density target when the boundary is coarser than the region it bounds
/// (flat margins tessellate coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on
/// a 946-vertex region). Sides with mismatched vertex counts fall back to
/// arc-length-uniform stations.
fn axis_stations(
    side_a: &[f64],
    side_b: &[f64],
    target: usize,
    profile: &[(f64, f64)],
) -> Vec<f64> {
    if side_a.len() != side_b.len() || side_a.len() < 2 {
        return AxisMaps::stations(profile, target);
    }
    let base: Vec<f64> = side_a
        .iter()
        .zip(side_b)
        .map(|(a, b)| ((a + b) / 2.0).clamp(0.0, 1.0))
        .collect();
    let k = (target.saturating_sub(1)).div_ceil(base.len() - 1).max(1);
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity((base.len() - 1) * k + 1);
    for w in base.windows(2) {
        for step in 0..k {
            out.push(w[0] + (w[1] - w[0]) * step as f64 / k as f64);
        }
    }
    out.push(*base.last().unwrap());
    out
}
```

**After:**
```
/// Grid stations for one axis. The base stations are the boundary
/// vertices' own pinned positions (averaged between the two opposite
/// sides — structured or not, the boundary is where the region has exact
/// on-surface data at exact parameters): stations *between* the vertices
/// of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the interpolant to
/// the chordal prism (a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own
/// chordal error). The base is then evenly subdivided up to the interior-
/// density target when the boundary is coarser than the region it bounds
/// (flat margins tessellate coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on
/// a 946-vertex region). Sides with mismatched vertex counts fall back to
/// arc-length-uniform stations.
///
/// Station spacing is CONDITIONED in 3D-arc measure (the axis profile):
/// chord-length-parametrized C2 interpolation amplifies data noise by
/// roughly the square of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio, so a boundary
/// mixing fine facets with one long straight chord (the xyz-cube letter
/// walls: a 21× jump) turns a 0.02 mm-flat ribbon into control points
/// 150 mm off the part — while passing through every grid node exactly.
/// Near-coincident stations (the other conditioning failure: a
/// near-singular system) are merged; every segment is then subdivided
/// until no piece exceeds GRADE_RATIO× the pieces of either neighbor
/// segment. Subdivision is uniform in arc, not UV (the Floater map
/// compresses high-curvature areas, so UV-uniform substations cluster
/// in 3D). Surviving boundary-vertex stations keep their exact pinned
/// positions; new substations land only inside long segments, where a
/// long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat and mid-segment
/// samples lie on the true surface (the arch's chordal-prism deficit
/// came from stations between vertices on CURVED stretches).
fn axis_stations(
    side_a: &[f64],
    side_b: &[f64],
    target: usize,
    profile: &[(f64, f64)],
) -> Vec<f64> {
    if side_a.len() != side_b.len() || side_a.len() < 2 {
        return AxisMaps::stations(profile, target);
    }
    /// Merge stations closer (in arc) than this fraction of the median gap.
    const MERGE_FRAC: f64 = 0.25;
    /// Max allowed piece size relative to either neighbor segment's pieces.
    const GRADE_RATIO: f64 = 2.0;
    /// Hard cap on stations per axis (cost guard; the control-point bound
    /// check in `fit_grid` still protects if grading is cut short).
    const MAX_STATIONS: usize = 257;

    // (uv, arc-fraction) base stations.
    let base: Vec<(f64, f64)> = side_a
        .iter()
        .zip(side_b)
        .map(|(a, b)| {
            let uv = ((a + b) / 2.0).clamp(0.0, 1.0);
            (uv, AxisMaps::arc_fraction(profile, uv))
        })
        .collect();

    // Merge pass: drop interior stations far closer than the median gap to
    // their kept predecessor (corner stations always survive).
    let mut gaps: Vec<f64> = base.windows(2).map(|w| w[1].1 - w[0].1).collect();
    gaps.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
    let median_gap = gaps[gaps.len() / 2].max(f64::EPSILON);
    let merge_tol = MERGE_FRAC * median_gap;
    let mut kept: Vec<(f64, f64)> = vec![base[0]];
    for &s in &base[1..base.len() - 1] {
        if s.1 - kept.last().unwrap().1 >= merge_tol {
            kept.push(s);
        }
    }
    let last = *base.last().unwrap();
    if kept.len() > 1 && last.1 - kept.last().unwrap().1 < merge_tol {
        kept.pop();
    }
    kept.push(last);

    // Per-segment piece counts: the uniform density target, then graded
    // subdivision to a fixpoint so no piece exceeds GRADE_RATIO× the
    // (current) pieces of either neighbor segment.
    let seg_arc: Vec<f64> = kept
        .windows(2)
        .map(|w| (w[1].1 - w[0].1).max(f64::EPSILON))
        .collect();
    let k = (target.saturating_sub(1)).div_ceil(seg_arc.len()).max(1);
    let mut pieces: Vec<usize> = vec![k; seg_arc.len()];
    for _ in 0..32 {
        let mut changed = false;
        for i in 0..seg_arc.len() {
            let eff = |j: usize| seg_arc[j] / pieces[j] as f64;
            let mut cap = f64::INFINITY;
            if i > 0 {
                cap = cap.min(GRADE_RATIO * eff(i - 1));
            }
            if i + 1 < seg_arc.len() {
                cap = cap.min(GRADE_RATIO * eff(i + 1));
            }
            if !cap.is_finite() {
                continue;
            }
            let total: usize = pieces.iter().sum();
            let headroom = MAX_STATIONS.saturating_sub(total + 1);
            let want = ((seg_arc[i] / cap).ceil() as usize).min(pieces[i] + headroom);
            if want > pieces[i] {
                pieces[i] = want;
                changed = true;
            }
        }
        if !changed {
            break;
        }
    }

    // Emit: kept stations at their exact pinned uv; substations uniform in
    // arc within each segment, mapped back through the profile.
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(pieces.iter().sum::<usize>() + 1);
    for (w, &np) in kept.windows(2).zip(&pieces) {
        out.push(w[0].0);
        for step in 1..np {
            let t = w[0].1 + (w[1].1 - w[0].1) * step as f64 / np as f64;
            out.push(uv_at_arc(profile, t).clamp(w[0].0, w[1].0));
        }
    }
    out.push(kept.last().unwrap().0);
    out
}

/// Invert an axis profile: the uv whose arc fraction is `t` (piecewise-
/// linear interpolation of the profile's (uv, arc) samples).
fn uv_at_arc(profile: &[(f64, f64)], t: f64) -> f64 {
    let k = profile
        .partition_point(|&(_, g)| g < t)
        .clamp(1, profile.len() - 1);
    let (u0, g0) = profile[k - 1];
    let (u1, g1) = profile[k];
    let s = if g1 > g0 {
        ((t - g0) / (g1 - g0)).clamp(0.0, 1.0)
    } else {
        0.0
    };
    u0 + s * (u1 - u0)
}
```
</details>

```
/// positions; new substations land only inside long segments, where a
/// long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat there and
/// mid-segment samples lie on the true surface (the arch's chordal-prism
/// deficit came from stations between vertices on CURVED stretches).
fn axis_stations(
    side_a: &[f64],
    side_b: &[f64],
    target: usize,
    profile: &[(f64, f64)],
) -> Vec<f64> {
    if side_a.len() != side_b.len() || side_a.len() < 2 {
        return AxisMaps::stations(profile, target);
    }
    /// Merge stations closer (in arc) than this fraction of the median gap.
    const MERGE_FRAC: f64 = 0.25;
    /// Max allowed piece size relative to either neighbor segment's pieces.
    const GRADE_RATIO: f64 = 2.0;
    /// Hard cap on stations per axis (cost guard; the control-point bound
    /// check in `fit_grid` still protects if grading is cut short).
    const MAX_STATIONS: usize = 257;

    // (uv, arc-fraction) base stations.
    let base: Vec<(f64, f64)> = side_a
        .iter()
        .zip(side_b)
        .map(|(a, b)| {
            let uv = ((a + b) / 2.0).clamp(0.0, 1.0);
            (uv, AxisMaps::arc_fraction(profile, uv))
        })
        .collect();

    // Merge pass: drop interior stations far closer than the median gap to
    // their kept predecessor (corner stations always survive).
    let mut gaps: Vec<f64> = base.windows(2).map(|w| w[1].1 - w[0].1).collect();
    gaps.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
    let median_gap = gaps[gaps.len() / 2].max(f64::EPSILON);
    let merge_tol = MERGE_FRAC * median_gap;
    let mut kept: Vec<(f64, f64)> = vec![base[0]];
    for &s in &base[1..base.len() - 1] {
        if s.1 - kept.last().unwrap().1 >= merge_tol {
            kept.push(s);
        }
    }
    let last = *base.last().unwrap();
    if kept.len() > 1 && last.1 - kept.last().unwrap().1 < merge_tol {
        kept.pop();
    }
    kept.push(last);

    // Per-segment piece counts: the uniform density target, then graded
    // subdivision to a fixpoint so no piece exceeds GRADE_RATIO× the
    // (current) pieces of either neighbor segment.
    let seg_arc: Vec<f64> = kept
        .windows(2)
        .map(|w| (w[1].1 - w[0].1).max(f64::EPSILON))
        .collect();
    let k = (target.saturating_sub(1)).div_ceil(seg_arc.len()).max(1);
    let mut pieces: Vec<usize> = vec![k; seg_arc.len()];
    for _ in 0..32 {
        let mut changed = false;
        for i in 0..seg_arc.len() {
            let eff = |j: usize| seg_arc[j] / pieces[j] as f64;
            let mut cap = f64::INFINITY;
            if i > 0 {
                cap = cap.min(GRADE_RATIO * eff(i - 1));
            }
            if i + 1 < seg_arc.len() {
                cap = cap.min(GRADE_RATIO * eff(i + 1));
            }
            if !cap.is_finite() {
                continue;
            }
            let total: usize = pieces.iter().sum();
            let headroom = MAX_STATIONS.saturating_sub(total + 1);
            let want = ((seg_arc[i] / cap).ceil() as usize).min(pieces[i] + headroom);
            if want > pieces[i] {
                pieces[i] = want;
                changed = true;
            }
        }
        if !changed {
            break;
        }
    }

    // Emit: kept stations at their exact pinned uv; substations uniform in
    // arc within each segment, mapped back through the profile.
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(pieces.iter().sum::<usize>() + 1);
    for (w, &np) in kept.windows(2).zip(&pieces) {
        out.push(w[0].0);
        for step in 1..np {
            let t = w[0].1 + (w[1].1 - w[0].1) * step as f64 / np as f64;
            out.push(uv_at_arc(profile, t).clamp(w[0].0, w[1].0));
        }
    }
    out.push(kept.last().unwrap().0);
    out
}

/// Invert an axis profile: the uv whose arc fraction is `t` (piecewise-
/// linear interpolation of the profile's (uv, arc) samples).
fn uv_at_arc(profile: &[(f64, f64)], t: f64) -> f64 {
    let k = profile
        .partition_point(|&(_, g)| g < t)
        .clamp(1, profile.len() - 1);
    let (u0, g0) = profile[k - 1];
    let (u1, g1) = profile[k];
    let s = if g1 > g0 {
        ((t - g0) / (g1 - g0)).clamp(0.0, 1.0)
    } else {
        0.0
    };
    u0 + s * (u1 - u0)
}
```

```markdown
5. **Resample a grid** through the flattened triangulation (uniform-bin point location; numeric misses near corner slivers are clamped to the nearest triangle within one bin — feeding them to the hole membrane extrapolates garbage). Grid stations per axis are the boundary vertices' own pinned positions (stations *between* the vertices of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the fit to the chordal prism — a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own chordal error), subdivided up to an interior-density target of ~1.5·√(region vertices) (a flat margin tessellates coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on a 946-vertex region). Station spacing is **conditioned in 3D-arc measure** (`axis_stations`): chord-length-parametrized C2 interpolation amplifies data noise by roughly the square of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio, so a boundary mixing fine facets with one long straight chord (the xyz-cube letter walls: a 21× jump) turned a 0.02 mm-flat ribbon into control points 150 mm off the part while passing through every grid node exactly. Near-coincident stations (< 0.25× the median arc gap — the near-singular end of the same conditioning problem) are merged, and segments are subdivided to a fixpoint where no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor segment's pieces, uniformly in arc rather than UV (the Floater map compresses high-curvature areas). Surviving boundary-vertex stations keep their exact pinned positions; new substations land only inside long segments, where a long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat and mid-segment samples lie on the true surface. Genuine hole interiors are filled with a Laplacian membrane so the fitter sees smooth data. The grid is flipped if needed so d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always `concave = false`.
```

```
---
\`compute_tangent_edge_curve_line\` originally assumed plane-CYLINDER geometry: direction = cylinder axis, point at radius_at_apex from the axis. For a true cone the tangent line is a GENERATOR (tilted from the axis by the half-angle), and radius_at_apex = 0 collapses the analytic point onto the axis — the constructed line ran through the apex, 11 mm off. The two endpoint vertices lie exactly on the generator, so cones use the vertex-to-vertex line directly.

### Complete tori use natural-bounds face construction

A complete donut selects one toroidal surface with NO boundary edges; like full spheres, the face is built with \`MakeFace::new_handlegeomsurface_real\` (natural [0,2π]×[0,2π] bounds), which creates both the U seam (around the major circle) and the V seam (around the tube) automatically. full_torus (tests/occt/, generated by gen-test-models via BRepPrimAPI_MakeTorus) passes stage 4.1 with volume matching to ~7e-9 relative.

### Curvature-guided seeding (stages 1+2) and toroidal-before-conical ordering

- \`compute_seed_scores\` (stage2/mod.rs) builds two per-face signals once per mesh: a diffused curvature-difference score for ORDERING (BFS start faces ascending — interior faces of uniform regions first) and a raw-score junction mask for GATHERING EXCLUSION (pass 1 only; pass 2 lifts the filter so all-junction surfaces still seed). Curvature summary per face = max over edges of dihedral/centroid-distance; the junction criterion is LOCAL relative change (score > 50% of local curvature magnitude) — a mesh-global threshold fails because sharp creases dominate the max and tangent junctions (the primary target, G1-continuous but G2-discontinuous) fall under any fraction of it.
- With clean interior seeds available, TOROIDAL detection (2.5) moved before CONICAL (2.2), completing the ordering principle: spheres → tori → cones/cylinders, most-constrained surfaces first. Any adjacent pair of quad rows on a torus tube lies exactly on some cone, so cones previously tiled fillets with 2-ring bands before the torus stage ran. Cylinders resist bogus torus fits cheaply (tube centers collapse onto the axis; the (h,r) profile is a constant-r line). Conical seeding skips torus-committed faces like sphere-committed ones.
- Toroidal seed gathering uses hop-expansion (up to 3 hops, target ≥16 faces) instead of a fixed 1-ring: small fillets (r=0.5 tubes, 2-3 rows) have little interior after junction exclusion across the tube but extend far along the fillet axis.
- Raw-hypothesis stage-2 compare failures are now warnings; the hard gate is the post-selection compare. Spurious pre-selection hypotheses are expected by design.
- Results: filleted_pipe stage 2.2 5.2 s → 0.06 s with exactly the right selection (its STEP has 4 tori — the previous "6 tori" selection was over-segmented); stepped_block stage 2.2 3 s → 0.4 s with planar count matching STEP exactly (17); filleted_cylinder 2.2+2.5 ≈ 0.03 s total.

### Boundary refinement and corner vertex refinement (plan §2.6 refitting)

- \`refine_boundary_assignments\` (stage 2.6, after absorption): region-boundary faces move to an adjacent selected surface that fits their vertices ≥2× better; iterated to a fixpoint. Fixes row-off region boundaries that shifted reconstructed corners (stepped_block fine's corner vertex went from 0.07 mm off to exact). Only membership changes — geometry is not re-fitted.
- \`refine_corner_vertices\` (stage 3, after tangency detection): SHARP corner vertices are refined by cyclic projection onto adjacent fitted surfaces. Must skip corners adjacent to tangent edges: tangent pairs meet along a curve, not a point, and cyclic projection slides along it (drifted 0.57 mm before the restriction). Guard: revert if the move exceeds 2× surface tolerance.
- Torus tangent ARC construction now tries both candidate axes (torus + adjacent cone/cylinder/other-torus axis) like the closed-loop variant, picking the circle that best fits the boundary vertices. A wrong axis projects both endpoint vertices to the same circle parameter → zero-span arc → MakeEdge "Differentpointsonclosedcurve".
- The tangent-edge/vertex compare relaxations must stay: with refinement, most models pass at tight tolerance, but genuinely ambiguous junction rows remain (a face whose vertices sit on both tangent surfaces equally is not movable by any best-fit rule), e.g. one edge on stepped_block fine at 0.044 mm.

### Medium-resolution tessellation: coverage floor, cylinder-only gating, and the default angular tolerance

stepped_block_toroidal_fillets MEDIUM (2.3K triangles) went from one undetected corner-blend torus (fails stage 3.1 at 0.35 mm) to full stage-4.1 pass via three coupled changes. The root cause is that Onshape's "medium" preset tessellates by chordal deviation, so a small feature gets very few segments regardless of the angular preset — an r=0.5 fillet becomes four rows across each 90° tube arc, with ~24° dihedral steps between rows.

- **BFS final re-fit keeps the better of two fits, not always the fresh one.** The end-of-trial \`S::fit_surface\` from scratch can converge to a *worse* local minimum than the fit maintained incrementally during BFS growth, even when every trial vertex is within 1e-5 of the incremental fit (observed: the full-ring R=3.5 pocket-rim fillet torus refit to 0.87 mm and was rejected). \`current_fit\` is a valid fallback by construction (it satisfied the vertex tolerance for every accepted face), so adopt the from-scratch fit only when it lowers the trial's max vertex error. This alone recovered the 18th torus.
- **Angular-coverage floor for coarse tessellation, gated to cylinders AND to well-spanned arcs.** The \`angular_coverage_valid\` span/3 second-gap rule rejects legitimate coarse fillets (four rows across 90° = 24° gaps > span/3 = 23°). Added a \`max_gap\` floor = \`angular_tol * angular_tol_multiplier\` (capped at span/2). Two gates keep it from admitting bogus bands:
  - *Cylinders only* (\`half_angle < 1e-4\`): a true cone's coarse-coverage lookalike is a 6-face ~50° half-angle patch fitted to a corner sphere-blend, which *must* stay rejected or it wins selection over the correct sphere (regressed rounded_cube_medium 48→40 tangent edges before the gate). Straight-edge fillets are cylinders, so gating loses nothing.
  - *Span ≥ 45° only*: without this, the floor floods a smooth truncated cone with spurious narrow cylinder bands — a thin slice of a shallow cone fits a cylinder within tolerance and is locally almost cylindrical (low n·axis variance, so the aggregate-normal check doesn't catch it). Instrumenting \`angular_coverage_valid\` showed the discriminator cleanly: the legit stepped_block corner fillet spans ~69°, every spurious nosecone_truncated band spans ≤14°. Without the span gate the floor regressed nosecone_truncated_medium (was passing) from 4 selected surfaces to 27 (over-segmented). 45° sits well above the bands and below any genuine coarse quarter/half-round.
- **Default --angular-tolerance raised 17.5° → 25°.** Conical seed-pair qualification (\`conical_pairwise_qualified\`) uses the *raw* (unmultiplied) angular tolerance, so the 24.1° fillet-row pairs were rejected below 25° and the four corner edge-fillet cylinders never seeded (33 vs 37 cylinders at 20° vs 25°). rounded_cube_10_r2_coarse's explicit \`--angular-tolerance=20\` still works; the selection for rounded_cube_medium is byte-identical at 17.5/20/25 (its fillet steps are finer). Verified no model regresses at the higher default (full suite green).
```
</details>

## 🤖🔧 Assistant

```bash
cat >> ./LEARNINGS.md <<'EOF'

### GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate blows up between nodes on uneven chord spacing

Chord-length-parametrized C2 cubic interpolation is ill-conditioned when adjacent data spacing jumps sharply: noise amplification grows roughly with the SQUARE of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio. On the xyz-cube letter walls (grid stations pinned to boundary vertices mixing 0.175 mm facet chords with one 3.66 mm straight chord — a 21× jump), a ribbon flat to 0.02 mm interpolated to a surface with control points 150 mm off the part — while `IsDone()` returned true, the residual at every grid node was ~1e-14, and per-vertex/per-centroid validation passed (all those sample at or near data points; the excursion lives BETWEEN nodes). Two defenses in stage 2.7: `axis_stations` conditions station spacing in 3D-arc measure (merge stations closer than 0.25× the median gap; subdivide, uniformly in arc, until no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor's pieces), and `fit_grid` rejects any fit with a control point outside the grid data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal (the surface lies in its poles' convex hull, so poles near the data bound the surface near the data — legit fits, including periodic bands, stay well inside).
EOF
git diff --stat
```
</details>

```bash
Stage 2.7: condition grid-station spacing; reject fits with runaway poles

Nearly-flat regions (the xyz-cube test model's embossed letter walls,
flat to 0.02 mm) fitted to B-spline surfaces with control points up to
150 mm outside the 20 mm part, importing as missing letters and slivers
shooting far beyond the cube. Root cause: `axis_stations` pins grid
stations at boundary-vertex positions, and that boundary mixes fine
facet chords (0.175 mm) with one long straight chord (3.66 mm) — a 21×
spacing jump fed into chord-length-parametrized C2 interpolation, whose
noise amplification grows roughly with the square of the adjacent
knot-spacing ratio. The fit passes through every grid node exactly
(~1e-14 residual) and every vertex/centroid validation sample, so the
inter-node excursion shipped silently.

Two fixes, prevention and detection:

- `axis_stations` now conditions station spacing in 3D-arc measure
  (via the existing AxisMaps profile): near-coincident stations
  (< 0.25x the median arc gap — the near-singular end of the same
  conditioning problem) are merged, and segments are subdivided to a
  fixpoint where no piece exceeds 2x either neighbor segment's pieces.
  Subdivision is uniform in arc rather than UV (the Floater map
  compresses high-curvature areas). Surviving boundary-vertex stations
  keep their exact pinned positions, preserving the arch rule; new
  substations land only inside long segments, where a long boundary
  chord means the mesh itself is flat there.

- `fit_grid` rejects any fit with a control point outside the grid
  data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal. The surface lies
  in the convex hull of its poles, so poles near the data bound the
  whole surface near the data; node-sampling validation is structurally
  blind to inter-node excursions, and this check closes that hole for
  every input, not just this one (rejection falls through to the
  existing density-retry ladder / single-face planar fallback).

xyz-cube results: all six letter-wall fits now keep every pole inside
the region bounds; max vertex deviation 0.036 mm -> 5e-7 mm, centroid
```

Committed as `5212e76`. Here's the summary:

## What I changed

**Root cause, sharpened:** it wasn't just "uneven stations" generically — chord-length-parametrized C2 interpolation amplifies data noise by roughly the **square** of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio. The letter-wall boundaries mix fine facet chords (0.175mm) with one long flat chord (3.66mm), a 21× jump; 21² ≈ 440× amplification of the mesh's 0.02–0.3mm flatness noise is exactly the observed 150mm pole excursion. The fit passes through every grid node to 1e-14 and every vertex/centroid validation sample, so nothing downstream could see it.

**Fix 1 — prevention** ([axis_stations](src/stage2/bspline.rs:1493)): station spacing is now conditioned in 3D-arc measure using the existing `AxisMaps` profile. Stations closer than 0.25× the median arc gap are merged (the near-singular end of the same conditioning problem), and segments are subdivided to a fixpoint where no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor's — graded spacing, uniform in arc rather than UV. Boundary vertices that survive keep their exact pinned positions, so the arch chordal-prism rule is intact; new mid-segment stations only appear inside *long* segments, where a long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat.

**Fix 2 — detection** ([fit_grid](src/stage2/bspline.rs:1725)): any fit with a control point outside the grid data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal is rejected. Since a B-spline surface lies in the convex hull of its poles, this bounds the *whole* surface near the data — closing the between-nodes blind spot for every future input, not just this one. Rejection falls through to the existing density-retry ladder / planar fallback.

## Verification

- **xyz-cube**: all six fits now keep every pole inside their region's bounds. Max vertex deviation 0.036mm → **5e-7mm**; centroid 0.34mm → 0.0015mm; STEP surface area 2696 → **2471.7mm²** (STL: 2471.7 — the 9% sliver excess is gone). All five IntSS edge failures, all eight BRepCheck face failures, and the solid warning **disappeared** — with sane surfaces, the intersections just work.
- **Full suite**: 476/476 green (~3¼ min), clippy clean.
- Docs updated (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.7, LEARNINGS.md gotcha entry) and included in the commit per AGENTS.md.

Two follow-ups you may want later: the near-planar gate is still 25–50× too tight to route these flat regions to planes (they're now *good* B-splines, but a CAD user would prefer 6 planes), and OrcaCube v2.stl fails stage 1.2 with 4 non-manifold edges — a separate, pre-existing limitation.

```
/// Stage 1.3: Fuse adjacent coplanar triangle pairs into quads.
///
/// For each manifold edge shared by two triangles, if both triangles are coplanar
/// (all 4 vertices within \`coplanar_tol\` of the shared plane) and their merged shape
/// forms a convex quad, merge them into a single quad face. Each triangle can
/// participate in at most one merge (greedy, first valid merge wins).
pub fn fuse_coplanar_triangles(mesh: &mut ConnectedMesh, vertex_tolerance: f64) {
    let coplanar_tol = vertex_tolerance;
    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();
    let mut merged = vec![false; num_faces];

    // Phase 1: Identify merge pairs.
    // Each entry: (face1_idx, edge1_idx, face2_idx, edge2_idx)
    let mut merge_pairs: Vec<(usize, usize, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();

    for fi in 0..num_faces {
        if merged[fi] {
            continue;
        }
        if mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count != 3 {
            continue;
        }
        let norm = match mesh.faces[fi].normal {
            Some(n) => n,
            None => continue,
        };

        for ei in 0..3_usize {
            let ni = mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[ei];
            if ni < 0 {
                continue;
            }
            let ni = ni as usize;
            // Process each edge only once and skip already-merged faces
            if ni <= fi || merged[ni] {
                continue;
            }
```

<!-- msg_011Cd5saaNNx6vStLPuAEwiZ -->

Before writing this up, I ran one more probe that corrects an important detail from my earlier analysis — it changes which options actually work.

**A sharper look at the "noise":** the per-facet normals along each letter wall aren't wobbling randomly — they progress *monotonically* (e.g. one X-stroke wall: 0.413 → 0.420 → 0.430 → 0.439 → 0.444 → 0.447 → 0.449 → 0.450 → 0.452 in one normal component). The walls are coherently bowed by ~2–3.7° end to end — a deliberate (or authoring-tool-induced) slight curl in the letter strokes, tessellated under chordal control, with a sagitta of 0.017–0.028mm. Adjacent triangle pairs are coplanar to 5e-5 (that's why stage 1.3 fused them into quads), but successive quads each tilt ~0.25°. So these regions are *truly, measurably non-planar* — just at a scale nobody printing a 20mm cube could ever care about. That reframes the question: this isn't noise detection, it's a **fidelity-threshold policy** — below what deviation do we prefer the simpler surface over the honest one?

I also ran the zero-code experiment: `--vertex-tolerance 0.03` on this model. Result: stage 2.1 absorbs the letter walls directly into multi-face planes — 49 surfaces total (45 planes + 4 cylinders, **zero B-splines**), stage 2.7 never fires, all faces valid, volume error 0.026%, area matches the STL to 0.02%. So one existing CLI flag already produces the all-planar letters you expected.

## The options

**A. Widen the stage 2.7 planarize gate, sized off surface tolerance.** The existing gate ([bspline.rs:2149](src/stage2/bspline.rs:2149)) becomes e.g. `max(10 × vertex_tol, surface_tol / 8)` — 0.05mm at defaults. Each of the six letter-wall regions gets one multi-face plane instead of a B-spline. One line, contained to leftover freeform regions, cheap (the plane fit already runs). The risk it was originally guarded against — "a shallow dome must not be flattened" — is quantifiable: only features with total sagitta under 0.05mm ever flatten, and the real freeform test models (hills, saddles, waves) have mm-scale height, orders of magnitude above the gate. What it *doesn't* do: the 32 sub-4-face fragments at stroke junctions stay as separate tiny planar faces, and it only helps regions that reach stage 2.7 at all.

**B. Raise `--vertex-tolerance` per model (works today, no code).** Measured above. This is the most "root-cause" framing: the pipeline's analytic detectors all run at vertex tolerance, which is calibrated for CAD-exact meshes (5e-5); this mesh's actual surface fidelity is ~0.03mm, so telling the pipeline that fixes everything upstream — stage 2.1 finds the walls as planes and 2.7 has nothing left to do. As a *default* it's risky (on exact meshes, looser vertex gates admit bogus analytic fits and shift selection competition), but as a documented per-model knob it's free. Downside: the user has to know the mesh's noise scale and reach for the flag.

**C. Auto-detect mesh fidelity in stage 1 and derive the effective vertex tolerance.** The automatic version of B: estimate the mesh's intrinsic exactness (e.g. the distribution of dihedral steps across edges the angular gate considers smooth, or the residual of a large planar region fit at loose tolerance) and scale the fitting tolerances accordingly. This is the right long-term answer for arbitrary downloaded/scanned meshes — the current default silently assumes CAD-exact input. It's also the most work and needs a trustworthy estimator: a genuinely curved fine tessellation (a sphere at high resolution) has small dihedral steps *by curvature*, not noise, so the estimator must separate the two (e.g. only sample within regions an analytic fit already accepted). Highest value, highest risk of regressions if the estimator misjudges.

**D. A post-selection "demote to plane" simplify pass.** After selection (or after 2.7), refit every selected surface's vertices against a best-fit plane; if max deviation ≤ a simplify tolerance, replace the surface with the plane. Same policy threshold as A but placed as a general mechanism: it would also catch the classic analytic accidents — a huge-radius cylinder or torus standing in for what is practically a plane — which option A never sees. Could hang off a new `--simplify-tolerance` knob (default = A's value, or off). Slightly more plumbing than A (the hypothesis lists and `face_to_selected` bookkeeping), and it changes reconstructed edges from curve-intersections to plane-intersections, so the compare gates would exercise it well.

**E. A structure test (coherent curvature vs. incoherent noise) — honestly, the wrong tool here.** I initially proposed distinguishing "flat with noise" from "genuinely curved" by fitting a quadric trend over the plane residuals and checking whether deviation is spatially coherent. It's principled, but the new measurement above shows it would do the *opposite* of what you want on this model: the letter walls have textbook-coherent monotone curvature, so a structure test keeps them as B-splines. It only makes sense combined with A if you wanted to preserve tiny-but-real features on exact meshes — and those are already protected by choosing the absolute cap conservatively.

## How they interact, and what I'd do

A and D are the same policy at different altitudes ("below X mm of flatness, prefer a plane"); B and C are the same policy at different automation levels ("tell/learn the mesh's true fidelity"). They compose: C fixes detection for noisy meshes generally, while A/D express a simplification preference that's desirable *even on exact meshes* (a 0.02mm-bowed wall in an exact mesh should still arguably export as a plane).

My recommendation: do **A** now — one line, contained, delivers 6 planes on this model, and the suite gates will confirm no exact-mesh regressions. Document **B** in the README as the workaround for visibly noisy meshes. Treat **D** as the follow-up if you ever see the huge-radius-cylinder flavor of the same disease, and **C** as the eventual root fix if real-world meshes become a primary input. Want me to implement A (plus the README note for B)?
